| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| No. | Share | Seats won | ± |  |  |  |
| [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_Kingdom_general_election) | [Nigel Farage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigel_Farage) | 4,117,610 | 14.3% | 5 / 650 | Increase 5 | Opposition |

### Local government

The party first stood at local government level in two by-elections in [Gloucester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gloucester) on 25 July 2019.[^cite_note-528] They did not win either.[^cite_note-529]

A councillor elected to [Rochdale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rochdale_Borough_Council) defected to the party in July 2019 from Labour, making for the first councillor;[^cite_note-530][^cite_note-531] shortly after a [Liberal Democrat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_Democrats_\(UK\)) councillor there also defected.[^cite_note-532] All 12 of [Rotherham's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotherham_Metropolitan_Borough_Council_elections) then UKIP councillors defected to the Brexit Party in July 2019, as did all 5 of [Derby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derby_City_Council)'s UKIP councillors.[^cite_note-533][^cite_note-534] On 13 September 2019, ten independent councillors on [Hartlepool Borough Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hartlepool_Borough_Council) defected to the Brexit Party. They then formed a pact with the three Conservatives to hold 13 of the 33 seats.[^cite_note-535] In September 2019, a Conservative councillor for [Surrey (county)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surrey_County_Council) and [Elmbridge (borough)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elmbridge_Borough_Council) defected to the party, after his party decided he would not be reselected.[^cite_note-536]

The 13 councillors of the Hartlepool council group left the party in 2020.[^cite_note-537] The Rotherham group left to form the Rotherham Democratic Party.[^cite_note-538][^cite_note-539] The party won two seats in the [2021 local elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_United_Kingdom_local_elections), both in [Derby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Derby_City_Council_election), one a hold from a previous defection and the other a gain. These were the first council seats won at election by the party, as all their previous ones had been via defections.[^cite_note-540][^cite_note-541] This left them with eight councillors in total; six in Derby and two more from defections, one in [Redbridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redbridge_London_Borough_Council) from the Conservatives, and one in [Swale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borough_of_Swale) from UKIP, both in April 2021.[^cite_note-542][^cite_note-543] Councillors in the Derby City group are members of an affiliate party named "[Reform Derby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_Derby)", in alignment with Reform UK.[^cite_note-544][^cite_note-545]

In December 2021, days before the [North Shropshire by-election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_North_Shropshire_by-election), local councillor and Deputy Mayor of [Market Drayton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_Drayton) Town Council, Mark Whittle, defected to the party from the Conservatives.[^cite_note-546] It was reported that all of Reform UK's candidates in the [2022 local elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_United_Kingdom_local_elections) "will campaign on the benefits of [fracking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fracking) and restarting exploration in the North Sea".[^cite_note-tele-new-brexit-353] Three of the eight council seats held by the party were up for re-election in 2022, all of which had arisen from defections. Both [Derby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Derby_City_Council_election) seats were held, but a seat in Redbridge was lost. No new seats were gained.[^cite_note-latest-547]

In December 2022, two former Conservative councillors – one in [Barnsley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barnsley_Metropolitan_Borough_Council) and the other in [West Oxfordshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Oxfordshire) – defected to the party.[^cite_note-548] Another Conservative councillor, Barry Gwilt, of the Fazeley ward of [Lichfield District Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lichfield_District#Political_control), defected to Reform UK in January 2023.[^cite_note-549] In the [2023 local elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_United_Kingdom_local_elections), Reform UK won six seats out of the 8,519 up for election[^cite_note-550] and averaged 6% of the vote in the wards where it stood.[^cite_note-551] The six seats won were all in the City of Derby, whose new council proceeded to elect Reform Derby leader Alan Graves to the position of Mayor for 2023/24.[^cite_note-552]

In March 2024, [East Riding of Yorkshire](153612.md) councillor Maria Bowtell defected from the Conservatives and joined the party.[^cite_note-553] In the [2024 local elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_Kingdom_local_elections), Reform UK took approximately 11% of the vote where it stood candidates,[^cite_note-554] and won two seats on [Havant Borough Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borough_of_Havant)[^cite_note-555] and one on the London Assembly.[^cite_note-London_Elects_2024-60] Richard Tice said that his party was becoming the real opposition to Labour.[^cite_note-556] On 18 June, four Conservatives from the [Tendring District Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tendring_District#Political_control) defected to Reform, with Jeff Bray becoming leader of the council group.[^cite_note-557]

Since the 2024 general election, Reform UK has won a number of council by-elections. Thirty-two councils now have at least one Reform UK councillor,[^cite_note-558] with the party winning by-elections in [Blackpool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blackpool_Council),[^cite_note-559] [Dartford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borough_of_Dartford),[^cite_note-560] [East Riding of Yorkshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Riding_of_Yorkshire_Council),[^cite_note-561] [Kent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kent_County_Council),[^cite_note-562] [St Helens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_Helens_Borough_Council),[^cite_note-563] [Swale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borough_of_Swale),[^cite_note-564] [Wolverhampton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Wolverhampton_Council),[^cite_note-565] and [Wyre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borough_of_Wyre).[^cite_note-566] On 10 January 2025, ten Reform councillors resigned from the party, saying that the party is being run in an "increasingly autocratic manner" since Farage's return as the party's leader.[^cite_note-JC-567] On 14 February 2025, Stuart Keyte became the first elected councillor for Reform UK in Wales, joining three other Reform councillors at Torfaen Council, who had defected to the party after previously sitting as independents.[^cite_note-568]

In March 2025, Reform UK gained defecting councillors in Scotland. John Gray from [Renfrewshire Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renfrewshire_Council) and Ross Lambie from [South Lanarkshire Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Lanarkshire_Council) both defected from the Conservative Party.[^cite_note-569] On 11 March 2025, [Falkirk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falkirk_\(council_area\)) councillor Claire Mackie-Brown also joined Reform UK from the Conservative Party.[^cite_note-570] Farage welcomed 29 defecting councillors at a press conference in [Westminster](37432.md). Of Reform UK's 113 council seats, 98 have come about via defections from politicians that were elected for another party – the majority, 66 from the Conservative Party – while 15 have been won through elections.[^cite_note-bbc-c871wzx8r47o-94] Amid this the Councillor Maria Bowtell left the party.[^cite_note-571]

At the [2025 local elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_United_Kingdom_local_elections), Reform stood 1,706 candidates[^cite_note-572] representing 97.5% of all wards up for election. It went on to win 677 seats and a majority of seats on 10 councils. The party also won 2 of the 6 mayoral elections taking place, [Greater Lincolnshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Greater_Lincolnshire_mayoral_election) and [Hull and East Yorkshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Hull_and_East_Yorkshire_mayoral_election). A projected national vote share collated by the [BBC](19344654.md) put Reform on 30% of the vote slightly ahead of its position in opinion polls conducted immediately prior to the local elections.[^cite_note-573] After four of Reform's new councillors stepped down, the party were unable to retain three of those seats in the subsequent by-elections, losing two to the Conservatives and the other to the Liberal Democrats.[^cite_note-bbc-conservative-win-251][^cite_note-bbc-durham-250][^cite_note-durham-bye-253]

Data has shown that most of Reform UK's representation comes from those who have defected from the Conservative Party.[^cite_note-Barker-Singh-524]

At the [2026 local elections](79139854.md), Reform stood 4,821 candidates representing 95% of all wards up for election. It went on to win 1,453 seats and a majority of seats on 14 councils.
